GitHub keeps no history of a retracted reaction, so `AGENTS.md:577-580` and `:618-622` are right that these rest on the polling as recorded. Everything they are used to conclude is either independently confirmed by a surviving timestamp or explicitly labelled as taken on trust. ## Findings

### should-fix — the reactions recipe still lets a 👀 clear the merge gate, and the sentence justifying it is false

`AGENTS.md:478-484`.

&gt; That endpoint returns everyone's reactions and marks them all `+1`

The endpoint does not do that. Three paragraphs earlier the file says so correctly: "`content` reading `eyes` and `+1` rather than the emoji." A 👀 comes back as `"content":"eyes"`; only thumbs-ups come back as `+1`, whoever left them. The true statement is the narrower one — a human's 👍 is *indistinguishable by content* from the bot's, which is why the filter has to be on `user.login`.

That matters because the selector offered as "the whole of it" filters on user alone:

```
--jq '.[] | select(.user.login == "chatgpt-codex-connector[bot]")'
```

Concrete failure, entirely inside the timings this same section measured: an agent pushes, the bot puts 👀 up five to ten seconds later (both #104 pushes, per the section), the agent polls, and the selector returns one object with `created_at` newer than the push. A reader who took "marks them all `+1`" at its word has no reason to inspect `content`, concludes the gate is satisfied, and merges two to five minutes before the review that is currently running would have posted its findings. That is the same shape as the hole I raised last round, arriving through the sentence written to close it.

Suggested fix: `select(.user.login == "chatgpt-codex-connector[bot]" and .content == "+1")`, and reword to something like "a human thumbs-up carries the same `+1` content as the bot's, so `content` cannot tell them apart and `user.login` is what must."

I also want to be straight about the confirmation you reported. The selector "returns the bot's reaction alone" on #104 — but `chatgpt-codex-connector[bot]` is the *only* account that has ever reacted to a pull request in this repository (I checked every PR), so that run filtered nothing out and could not have failed. The selector is correct on inspection; the check did not exercise it.

### should-fix — the surviving historical figure's ratio claim does not reproduce

`tests/instructions.sh:178-182`.

&gt; "The Codex review" measured 7954 in the commit that added this check, where a 4000 floor was half of it; it more than doubled over the two commits after, which left 4000 under a third without anything saying so.

Measured with the function's own awk: 7954 at `6ed975b` (correct, and 4000 is 50.3% of it — correct), 14138 in the worktree. That is ×1.78, not "more than doubled". And there are no commits after `6ed975b` on this branch; the growth is in the uncommitted change that carries this very comment. Only "under a third" survives (4000/14138 = 28%).

Failure scenario: this is the one number-bearing sentence you kept precisely because it was meant to be stable, and it is the sentence the next maintainer reads to decide how the floor drifts. Reproducing it and getting ×1.78 teaches them the comment is not checkable — the same lesson the paragraph below it exists to prevent.

Suggested fix: drop the multiplier and the commit count rather than restating them, e.g. "…where a 4000 floor was half of it; the change after it alone was enough to leave 4000 under a third, without anything saying so." That keeps the stable figure, keeps the moral, and adds no number that will be wrong next week.

### nit — two rewrap artifacts

`AGENTS.md:451` (43 columns, breaking `gh api` from `repos/...` without keeping anything intact) and `AGENTS.md:541` (50 columns, "…the same second for #102. So"). Everything else in the section wraps at 72 or under — I checked display width, not bytes; the mawk-flagged long lines are all emoji and em-dashes and are fine on screen.

## On the substitution you asked me to judge

Better, and not by a small margin — but not because measuring is cheap.

The one-liner reproduces exactly. I ran it verbatim, extracted from the comment with the `#` stripped, under both `sh` and `bash`: 14138, identical to `check_section`'s awk on the same file. The backslash-newlines inside the single-quoted program are awk line continuations rather than shell ones, which is a place this could easily have been broken by copying, and it is not. The only divergence from the function is `print b` versus `print b + 0`, which differs only for a section with an empty body — where the function's guard would catch a blank anyway.

The bytes/characters note is right and correctly scaled: mawk gives 14138 bytes here, a UTF-8 gawk would give 13951 characters, a difference of 187 against a 7638 margin above the floor.

What decides it for me is that the burden was already there and invisible. A quoted number puts a *false* thing in front of a reader who will not run anything, and the reader can't tell. The instruction to measure puts a *true* thing in front of them and makes the cost of not measuring visible. Finding 2 above is that argument's own evidence: the one number-shaped claim you kept is the only part that is wrong.

The gap it leaves is the one the comment names at lines 209-213 and then does nothing about — the drift condition is silent by construction, and "if you are reading this" only reaches someone already reading. The prompting version is a mechanism, not prose: a companion upper bound that fails when `body` exceeds some multiple of `minbody`, turning "the floor has fallen behind" into a message instead of a hope. That is a design change to the check rather than a defect in this one, and it belongs to a different slice — worth filing under step 9, not fixing here.

## Findings

### should-fix — the measurement recipe fails in exactly the situation it exists for

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tests/instructions.sh:190-191`

```
#   . tests/instructions.sh    # sources and runs; see AGENTS_BODY_AWK
#   awk -v h='### ' -v n='The Codex review' "$AGENTS_BODY_AWK" AGENTS.md
```

Sourcing runs the script to completion, and on any failure it reaches `exit 1` at line 357 — which, in a sourced script, exits the *caller's* shell. Line 2 of the recipe is never reached.

The reason that matters is that the recipe's own purpose (line 188: "Measure it instead") is re-aiming a floor, and the commonest moment to do that is right after the floor fired. Reproduced against a copy with the section gutted:

```
FAIL: AGENTS.md's "The Codex review" section is down to 6 characters, under the 6500 floor …
outer exit=1
```

`REACHED-NEXT-LINE` never printed, no number ever printed. In an interactive shell this closes the terminal rather than printing anything. The success path does work — I ran it and got `14426`, the same figure `check_section` compares — so the failure is silent and situational, which is the shape this file elsewhere goes to some length to avoid.

Two fixes, either fine:

- Smallest: say so in the comment — that the recipe only prints when every check passes, and that a failing run exits the shell you sourced into, so check out a passing revision or fix the other failures first.
- Better, and it keeps the single-copy property the comment is built around: give the script a measurement mode that uses `AGENTS_BODY_AWK` directly, e.g. `tests/instructions.sh . --body 'The Codex review'`, and name that in the comment instead of the sourcing dance. No second copy, no shell to lose.

### nit — sourcing also leaks `set -u` and `check_section`'s variables into the caller

`tests/instructions.sh:18`, `:239-241`

On the success path, sourcing leaves `set -u` on in the interactive shell (confirmed: `set -u LEAKED`), so the next typo referencing an unset variable ends the session too. POSIX `sh` has no `local`, so `name`, `why`, `minbody`, `body` and `points` land there as well. Both follow from the recipe rather than from the function, and the `--body` option above removes them along with the main problem.

### nit — the counting-units caveat is stated twice, and the message contradicts both

`tests/instructions.sh:199-201`, `:225-227`, `:257`

Lines 199-201 attribute the bytes-vs-characters split to the awk implementation ("bytes under mawk"), lines 225-227 attribute it to the locale ("characters under a UTF-8 locale, bytes under C"). Both are individually defensible and they say the same operative thing, in a comment block already 100 lines long over a 4-line program. The failure message at line 257 then asserts `"characters"` unconditionally. Collapsing the two paragraphs into one and softening the message to "units" or "characters (bytes in some awks)" would leave the block saying one thing.

## On the executable change you flagged

`AGENTS_BODY_AWK` through a shell variable is safe in POSIX `sh`. The value is single-quoted at definition so `$0` is not expanded, and `"$AGENTS_BODY_AWK"` at line 246 is double-quoted, so no field splitting, no globbing, and no second round of expansion — POSIX does not re-expand the result. Embedded newlines and tabs survive as one argument. It produced `14426` identically both as the variable and as an inline literal.

The `# shellcheck disable=SC2016` is correctly scoped. Removing it produces exactly one diagnostic, `SC2016` at line 231, and nothing else — so the directive suppresses only that assignment and is not masking anything, including the second, inline awk program in `check_section` (shellcheck recognises that one as an awk argument and does not flag its `$0`). `shellcheck tests/instructions.sh` is clean as committed.

I also broke both new checks to confirm they fire: gutting the section to a stub gave `down to 6 characters, under the 6500 floor`, and rewriting both forward pointers gave `nothing in AGENTS.md points forward at "The Codex review"`. Current bodies measure 1680 (floor 850, 50.6%) and 14426 (floor 6500, 45%), consistent with the "about half" rule the comment states. The `STANDARDS=4` exclusion is real: the phrase flattens to 4 occurrences, and gutting the Codex section did not change that count.

## Findings

### should-fix — `tests/instructions.sh:293`: the self-invocation has no recursion guard, so a broken `--body` dispatch hangs and leaks processes instead of failing

`check_section` re-enters the script with `sh "$0" "$ROOT" --body "$name"`, relying entirely on the child taking the `--body` branch at line 47 and leaving at the `exit 0` on line 66. If it does not — I tested it by deleting that `exit 0`, which is the same class of edit the other three mutations were — the child runs the full checks, reaches `check_section`, spawns another child, and so on without bound.

The failure mode is worse than a hang. Observed: a chain of `sh tests/instructions.sh . --body Answering a finding` processes each parented to the previous, still growing at ~800 processes after two minutes, and orphaned to PID 1 when the driver was killed. `ctest` sets `TIMEOUT 30` on this test, so CI would report a timeout while the chain kept forking after the test was reaped. Recovery is also awkward: killing a mid-chain process returns from the parent's command substitution, and the parent then goes on to the *second* `check_section` and starts a fresh chain — I had to restore the script before the kills would stick.

This matters precisely because the mutation that triggers it is the one this check exists to catch. Every other way of breaking `--body` produces a clear failure; the one that stops the dispatch firing produces a hang.

Suggested fix, two lines and it lands in the message the check already has. In `check_section`:

```sh
if ! flag=$(INSTRUCTIONS_BODY_CHILD=1 sh "$0" "$ROOT" --body "$name" 2&gt;&amp;1); then
```

and immediately after the `--body` block's `fi` at line 67:

```sh
# The child above is only ever invoked with --body, so getting here with
# the marker set means the dispatch stopped firing. Refuse rather than run
# the checks again: check_section would re-invoke, and so on -- which hangs
# instead of failing, and leaves the chain running after ctest's timeout
# kills the top of it.
if [ "${INSTRUCTIONS_BODY_CHILD:-}" = 1 ]; then
	printf '%s: --body did not dispatch; refusing to recurse\n' "$0" &gt;&amp;2
	exit 2
fi
```

On your other two questions, no defect: `sh "$0"` is the right re-entry — it survives a lost exec bit, and it resolved correctly under every invocation form I tried; and the two new exit statuses are coherent (2 for usage/missing file, 1 for a content failure, matching the script's own exit 1), with `check_section` treating any non-zero as "the recipe does not work".

### nit — `tests/instructions.sh:47-67`: nothing exercises the new guards

`check_section` drives only the happy path, so the empty-name guard, the missing-file guard and the missing-heading guard are new executable code that no run touches; a future edit that reordered them past the `awk` would go unnoticed, which is the exact hazard the empty-name guard was added for. One more assertion in `check_section` covers them for the price of the one already there — e.g. assert that `sh "$0" "$ROOT" --body ''` exits 2 — and would also cover the recursion guard above.

### nit — `tests/instructions.sh:61`: `--body` accepts a name that only matches as a regex, and answers `0`

`grep -q "^### $3"` treats the name as a BRE while the awk matches it literally, so a name with a metacharacter can pass the guard and measure nothing:

```
$ sh tests/instructions.sh . --body 'The C.dex review'
0
```

Exit 0, and a reader re-aiming a floor gets a number rather than an error. The comment at lines 178-182 already notes this divergence for `check_section`, where the names are literals under the author's control; here the input is a human's typing, which is what the guard beside it is for. Cheapest close: after the awk, refuse a measurement of 0 with the "no such section" message.

## Findings

### 1. should-fix — `AGENTS.md:417-418`: a second dangling reference the trim left behind, in the section this branch is already repairing pointers for

"Answering a finding" says:

&gt; A resolution alters no code, so it triggers no rerun: steps 6, 7 and 8 each condition theirs on a change, **and step 11 forbids repeating `code-review` over an unchanged worktree.**

`ba8d6e0` deleted the old step 11 ("Create pull requests from the reviewed state"), which carried "Do not repeat code review when the already-reviewed diff and worktree remain unchanged." The new step 11 is "Let Codex review the pull request, and answer it" and says nothing about reruns. So this is the same defect as the step 12 clause you are fixing, in the same file, from the same trim — and worse, because the rule is not merely mispointed but **gone from the file entirely**: steps 6, 7 and 8 state only the positive rerun conditions, and nothing anywhere now states that an unchanged worktree does not get a fresh pass.

Failure scenario: an agent resolving a `code-review` finding by argument reads "Answering a finding", follows it to step 11 for the rule its whole "where it goes" argument rests on, finds a Codex-review procedure instead, and is left to guess whether step 8's "obtain a fresh `code-review` approval for the changed state" obliges a rerun over a state that did not change. That is the exact ambiguity the deleted sentence existed to close.

Fix: repoint or restate. Since the rule no longer exists anywhere, repointing alone will not do — the minimal honest edit is to drop the clause and say the rule inline, e.g. "…steps 6, 7 and 8 each condition theirs on a change, and an unchanged worktree gets no repeat of any of them." If you would rather keep this slice to the guard removal, that is a legitimate explicit resolution — but then it has to be filed under step 9, not left silent, because the sentence is wrong as it stands.

### 2. should-fix — `tests/instructions.sh:133`: the restored check is an existence check, not a forward-pointer check, and that ordering was the one thing `#104` fixed rather than added

This answers your first question directly. Everything else the revert drops is the guard: `AGENTS_BODY_AWK`, the `--body` form, `probe_body`, the recursion marker, both floors, and the now-obsolete comment about "The Codex review" binding a fourth pass uncounted (correctly removed — with the section gone, `STANDARDS=4` is still the right number: step 6, step 7, step 8, and the definition at `AGENTS.md:405-406`).

The exception is the pointer check. `#104`'s `check_section` required the quoted mention to appear *before* the heading (`q &gt; 0 &amp;&amp; h &gt; 0 &amp;&amp; q &lt; h`); the restored `grep -q "\"$SECTION\""` accepts one anywhere. Its own comment in the removed code said why that mattered: "Counting quoted mentions was enough while 'Answering a finding' had one pointer and one heading, and stopped being enough the moment a later section quoted the name too."

I reproduced the hole against the current worktree — deleting step 6's pointer clause at `AGENTS.md:207` and adding a back-reference inside the section body passes with `exit 0`, and that is precisely the state the check exists to catch. It is not live today (only one quoted mention exists, at line 207, above the heading), because the second one lived in the section `ba8d6e0` deleted. But it is a capability regression relative to the state being reverted from, and it costs about ten lines with no floor, no `--body`, and no recursion to keep.

Fix: keep the ordering-aware awk inline (name, `q &lt; h`, the three-way `yes`/`no`/other guard) instead of the plain `grep`; or resolve explicitly and file it, noting that #105's prefix-match hole sits on the same check.

**should-fix — `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tests/instructions.sh:123-126`: the new comment states the opposite of what the code does.**

```
# is issue #105, and the pointer check below inherits nothing of it --
# that one uses awk's literal index().
```

The pointer check locates the heading with `h = index(all, "### " s)` — a substring search, so it is a prefix match too. What `index()` avoids relative to `grep`'s BRE is *metacharacter interpretation*, not prefix matching; the comment conflates the two. Demonstrated: rename the heading to `### Answering a finding here` and the whole script exits 0 with no FAIL — both the `grep` and the pointer check are satisfied while every pointer names a heading that is gone. Directly: `awk -v s='Answering a finding' '… index(all, "### " s)'` on a file whose only heading is `### Answering a finding here` returns `2`. The comment this replaced said it correctly ("All three matches here are prefix matches").

Failure scenario: whoever picks up #105 reads this comment, fixes only the `grep`, and leaves a second prefix matcher four lines below — and a reader auditing coverage credits the pointer check with a robustness it does not have. Suggested fix: say that both matchers are prefix matches and that `index()`'s literalness is a separate property, e.g. "…That is issue #105, and the pointer check below matches the same way — `index()` is literal rather than a BRE, but it is still a substring search, so a longer heading satisfies it too."

## 1. Verdict

**Acceptable as-is on the code; needs changes to the README paragraph.** The one-line change in `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c` does what it claims and does it well: I reproduced the ` , or frozen game?` and ` CONTINUE]` stumps on a `main` build, and on this branch every squeeze I tried — 20 sizes from 72x24 down to 10x8, single-column drags in both directions, rapid drag storms, and three-row-deep wrapped answers — brought the pending line back exactly once, whole, with the cursor at the right character and typing/backspacing continuing correctly. I found no artifact that restoring on a grow introduces: no duplicate question, no misplaced prompt, no overtyped answer, and plain mode is byte-identical to `main` on a deterministic tournament journey. Restoring on grow is in fact strictly better than `main` in a case the diff doesn't claim: a pending line that was wrapped mid-word by a narrow window gets re-joined onto one row when the window widens, where `main` leaves it split with a gap. The problem is the README: it promises a guarantee the game does not deliver for a whole class of prompts, and it removes the only recovery hint a player had for exactly those prompts.

## 2. Findings

### should-fix — `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/README.md`: the new paragraph overstates what comes back, and drops the escape hatch for the cases it doesn't cover

The new text says the restored line covers "the question waiting for an answer, or the pause waiting for a keystroke", and that "a corner drag **never** leaves you typing into the tail of a prompt, with the reader still sitting behind it eating your first keystroke."

`restore_curline()` bails on `curlen == 0`, and `note_out()` resets `curlen` on `\n`. So any prompt whose final line is printed with `prout` (which appends the newline) rather than `proutn` is invisible to the restore. That is at least ten prompts:

```
ai.c:527       "Mr. Spock-  \"Captain, shall we cancel the rest period?\""
battle.c:54    "...Treaty of Algeron is in effect.\n   Are you sure this is wise?\""
events.c:421   "Are you sure? "
finish.c:25    "SELF-DESTRUCT-SEQUENCE-WILL-BE-ABORTED"   (password read follows)
moving.c:503   "we dare spend the time?\""
moving.c:595   "  remaining time.  Are you sure this is wise?\""
planets.c:123  "Spock-  \"May I suggest the shuttle craft, Sir?\" "
planets.c:330  "Are you sure this is wise?\" "
planets.c:420  "  are you sure this is wise?\" "
sst.c:775      "[IMPORTANT ANNOUNCEMENT ARRIVING -- HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]"
```

Three verified live on this branch's binary at 80x24 → 20x8 → 80x24:

- `rest 100` → `Are you sure?` → corner drag → the pane comes back showing ` You will have 4 su` (an old banner line, cut mid-word), no prompt at all. Typing `srscan` produced ` srscan` then `Please answer with "Y" or "N":`. That is precisely the journey in issue #100, unchanged.
- The `pause(1)` announcement (forced via `debug` → set Supernova to 0.1 → `rest 0.5`) → corner drag → pane shows ` Make universe visi`, pause still pending. Typing `srscan` ate the `s` and the game asked `Super nova here?rscan` → `Please answer with "Y" or "N":`. The eaten first keystroke the paragraph says never happens.
- Self-destruct password prompt → corner drag → ` SELF-DESTRUCT-SEQU`, cut mid-word, with the game silently waiting for a password.

All three behave identically on `main`, so **this is not a regression** — the change neither causes nor worsens it. The defect is the documentation shipped alongside it. Compounding it, the diff deletes "If the prompt is not on screen with them, Enter brings it back," which was the one instruction that got a player out of these exact states.

Suggestion: narrow the claim to what the code guarantees and keep the recovery hint. Something like — "The line you are on usually comes back: a question or a pause that the game was part way through writing is written out again whole once there is room for it. A few prompts finish their line before they wait, and those are not rebuilt — if the pane comes back without a prompt on it, press Enter and the game will ask again." And drop the "never" sentence, or rewrite it without "the reader", which is implementation vocabulary a player has no referent for.

### nit — `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tests/tui.sh`: the new block locks in the question but not the pause

The new loop checks only the first setup question. The pause is the damaging half of #100 — the stump that is still pending and eats a keystroke — and the test's own comment says the announcement pause is out of reach because the suite passes setup answers on the command line. The pager pause is reachable: from `start 80 24 'tournament 7 short novice pw'`, `chart` at 80x24 stops at `[HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]` after ten lines, and I squeezed and restored it at 72x24, 40x24, 20x8, 10x8, 80x14 and 82x13, getting exactly one copy each time with the cursor at column 28. Adding that case would cover the `proutn` pause path the fix actually protects. (The existing block does earn its keep: run against the `main` binary it reports `FAIL: pending: after 20x8 …` and `after 10x8 …`, so it is not blind.)

### nit — resize behaviour is documented only in `README.md`, not in `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/sst.doc`

`sst.doc:1544-1547` describes `-t` and its 72x24 minimum but says nothing about resizing. A player who reaches the manual through the game never sees any of this. Pre-existing, untouched by the diff, and worth a line in the manual's `-t` paragraph at some point rather than in this slice.

### observation, no action — one line of transcript is overwritten on a wide-and-short shrink

At 80x24 with a full pane and an 80-character pending answer, shrinking to 78x22 replaced the two surviving history rows above the stump (`9 Klingons Left`, `10 Time Left`) with the rewritten line. That is the shrink path's `rows`-anchored clear, identical on `main`, and growing back to 80x24 added no further loss. Mentioning it only so it isn't mistaken for something the grow call introduced.

<result>## Verdict: request changes

Narrow ones. The one-line code change is correct and I verified it end-to-end; what needs fixing is comment accuracy in three places (this codebase treats comments as the spec, and one of them is now actively misleading) plus the review record step 8 asks for.

Reviewed state: branch `fix/tui-restore-pending-prompt` at `42c2a74` (identical to `main`; the branch carries **no commits**), plus the three uncommitted modified files — `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c`, `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tests/tui.sh`, `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/README.md`. Nothing staged, nothing untracked.

### What I verified rather than took on trust

- Builds clean with `-DSST_WERROR=ON`. `shellcheck -s sh tests/tui.sh` clean. `git diff --check` clean.
- **The test can fail.** I copied the worktree outside the repo, restored `tui.c` from `main`, configured a fresh `build/` there, and ran `tests/tui.sh` against it: exit 1 with exactly the four new assertions failing and nothing else — ` , or frozen game?` at 20x8, ` CONTINUE]` at 20x8, ` game?` and ` UE]` at 10x8. Against the fixed build the whole suite is `tui OK`, and `ctest -E tui` is 8/8 including `golden`.
- **The direction question you asked.** `COLS != oldcols` is right. The on-screen stump is always laid out at `builtcols` as it was when it was written, so `oldcols-2` is the layout width whichever way the width moved, and `rows = (total-1)/oldmsgw + 1` counts the rows it actually occupies in both directions. Live check on my own tmux socket, 100→76→100 with `COMMAND&gt; ` + 80 z's: after the shrink the pair is wrapped across two rows; after the grow it comes back reflowed onto one row, one `COMMAND&gt;`, 80 z's. That is the new `oldmsgw &gt; 0`-on-a-grow path doing the right thing.
- **Pure height change.** `oldmsgw == 0` is right: with the width unchanged nothing was re-laid-out, so there is no stump height to compute. In practice the branch is not reached, because the intact line is found first — checked live at 100x14, 100x20 and 40x30 from the setup question, one copy each time.
- **No doubling regression.** `tests/tui.sh:612` already grows (80x24 → 110x32) with `srsc` half-typed and asserts exactly one `COMMAND&gt;`; it passes. That is the exact failure the removed guard was written for, and it is covered.

### Findings

**should-fix — `tui.c:309-313`: the `cursor_at_prompt` comment still states the rule this change removes, and states it as a mechanism.**
"False when the restore was not needed at all -- a terminal that only grew -- where the cursor is still sitting after the answer and writing it again would double it." After the change a grow always calls `restore_curline()`, which sets the flag TRUE on both its exits; the only way it is left FALSE is the `curlen == 0` early return at `tui.c:368` — i.e. #112's `prout`-terminated prompts. Failure scenario: a maintainer reading this concludes grow is still excluded, and re-derives the doubled echo when reasoning about `tui_readline`'s re-echo. You rewrote the comment that justified the exclusion; this one justifies the same exclusion from the other end and was left. Fix: restate the FALSE case as "nothing was part written — a prompt that ended its own line (#112)".

**should-fix — `tui.c:706-711`: same staleness inside `tui_readline`'s `KEY_RESIZE` branch.**
"...and where the repaint did nothing at all, because the terminal only grew, the cursor is still after the answer and writing it again would double it." The behaviour is still correct (on a grow the found-path re-echo writes identical characters over themselves), but the stated reason is now wrong, and it is the comment that guards the `if (cursor_at_prompt &amp;&amp; len &gt; 0)` condition. Fix: say the repaint now runs whichever way the terminal moved, and that the guard exists for the case where there is no remembered line to move the cursor to.

**should-fix — `tests/tui.sh:1179-1181`: the justification for the 10x8 case is contradicted by the build it is meant to catch.**
"10x8 is past every minimum the display has, where the whole line went and nothing came back." Running the pre-fix build at 10x8 leaves ` game?` of the question and ` UE]` of the pause — a shorter stump, not nothing. (#100's table says "nothing at all" for 10x8, so the table is what is wrong; the comment inherited it.) Failure scenario: the next person reads this as the reason 10x8 covers something 20x8 does not, and cannot reproduce it. Fix: quote what the pre-fix build actually leaves at that size, e.g. "where the stump is down to ` game?` and ` UE]`", and consider correcting #100's table in a comment on the issue.

**nit — `tui.c:350-354` and `tui.c:421-423`: `restore_curline()`'s own comments still say "a terminal that loses columns".**
The `oldmsgw &gt; 0` branch is now reached on a width *gain* too, and the reason the arithmetic survives that — the stump was laid out at the old width whichever way it moved — lives only in `sync_size`'s new comment, not beside the arithmetic it justifies. One clause at `tui.c:421` would carry it.

**nit — `tests/tui.sh:1127-1129`: the older squeeze block's comment is now false.**
"what a squeeze leaves of the *pending* line is #100's subject and comes back a stump or not at all" — after this change it comes back whole. The block's reason for asking by typing is still sound; the sentence supporting it is not.

**nit — `tui.c:456-459`: the `else` branch anchors to `maxy-1`, which was equivalent to "the last row with anything on it" only while the call was restricted to shrinks.**
A grow can now reach it (width unchanged, line damaged at the smaller height), where the window has blank rows below the surviving conversation and the prompt would land at the bottom with a gap. I could not construct a live case: it needs `curlen + answer &gt; msgh * msgw` with `msgh &gt;= 2`, and the longest reachable pair (`COMMAND&gt; ` + a 126-char answer = 135) does not exceed the smallest such window (70 × 2 = 140). Latent, not live — but the `oldmsgw` branch's own comment at `tui.c:443-448` already argues why bottom-anchoring is wrong, and the same `last &gt;= 0 ? last : maxy - 1` would close it.

**nit — `README.md:48`: "Everything else is a command away" over-claims.**
The paragraph two lines above has just said scrolled-away conversation is gone for good, which no command brings back. The sentence you replaced — "Every reading is a command away" — was exact and did the same job.

**nit — `README.md:41` and `README.md:43` read as contradicting each other.**
"a line that was cut short stays cut short", then "The line you are on usually comes back ... written out again whole". A reader has to infer that the first means an older line. "an older line that was cut short stays cut short" fixes it.

**nit — `README.md:48`: "press Enter and the game asks again" is not what happens at the pause in that same category.**
`sst.c:775` prints `[IMPORTANT ANNOUNCEMENT ARRIVING -- HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]` with `prout` and then `getch()`, so it is one of the prompts the sentence is about — and Enter there dismisses the pause and clears the screen rather than asking anything again. Suggest "press Enter: a question is asked again, a pause moves on."

### On the review passes

`ui-review`'s should-fix and the mechanism behind it (`note_out()` clearing `curlen` at the newline, `restore_curline()` bailing on `curlen == 0`) is the kind of finding only a pass that actually drove the game produces, so I take it that the pass ran on this change. Two gaps in the record, per step 8:

1. You did not state, in the words step 8 asks for, whether the last `ui-review` round saw the state being committed. Reading the diff I believe no rerun was owed — the post-review edits were `README.md` and `tests/tui.sh`, neither of which is game output, and `tui.c` appears untouched since — but that is my inference from your account, not something you said. Say it explicitly, and give that round's verdict and the list of what it exercised, so the coverage can be checked against the four sizes the change actually affects.

My own runs (build with `-Werror`, `ctest -E tui` 8/8, `tests/tui.sh` green, pre-fix build failing exactly the four new checks) make that low-risk, but they are not that pass. If the verifier returns anything that changes code, step 8 wants a fresh review of the changed state.

The driver is not blind to the regression it was hunting: `&lt;sp&gt;/mutant2` is the end-state `tui.c` with the joined-row search cut back to one row (`int start = r;`), and it fails six of my checks with the exact historical artifact — two and then three copies of `Would you like a regular, tournament, or frozen game?bbbb…` stacked up the window. The joined search does make the grow-side restore safe across everything I threw at it.

### Missing coverage

**The grow-with-a-wrapping-pair case has no covering test.** `&lt;sp&gt;/mutant2` — the one-row search, which is the single thing standing between this change and the duplication it was once excluded to avoid — **passes the entire `tests/tui.sh` suite** (exit 0, "tui OK"). My driver catches it in seconds. The existing "resize typing" block grows 100x30→110x32 but with `srsc` typed, which never wraps; the wrapping cases (`shrink typing`, `shrink setup`) only shrink, and on a shrink a failed search is harmless because rewriting is correct anyway. Before this change the gap did not matter — a grow never called `restore_curline`. It does now. A `pending`-style block that types a wrapping answer and then *grows* would close it; it is a real assertion, since it fails on mutant2 and passes here.

### One comment claim that does not survive measurement

The new note in `restore_curline`'s `else` branch says of the bottom-anchoring gap: *"Not fixed here because it cannot be reached to be tested — getting in needs a pair longer than the whole message window, and the longest the game can produce … is shorter than the smallest window with two rows to scroll. Issue #114."*

I instrumented that branch (`&lt;sp&gt;/mutant4`, end-state `tui.c` plus a log line in each branch) and drove height-only resizes. The branch is reached routinely, by ordinary lines far shorter than the window:

```
SCROLL branch last=6 maxy=7 curline=[Would you like a regular, tournament, or frozen game?]
SCROLL branch last=6 maxy=7 curline=[[HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]]
SCROLL branch last=2 maxy=3 curline=[Would you like a regular, tournament, or frozen game?]
SCROLL branch last=0 maxy=1 curline=[COMMAND&gt; ]
```

A 53-character question in a seven-row window. So the stated reason is wrong as written. What I could *not* reproduce, in roughly forty height-only resizes across setup, command prompt and a `chart` pause, is the visible artifact: every entry had `last == maxy - 1`, so bottom-anchored and line-anchored coincided and no line was stranded above blank rows. The conclusion (leave it to #114) may well stand; the justification under it does not, and if #114 repeats it, the issue is misleading in the same way. Your call whether to reword the comment or the issue — I changed nothing.

<result>## Verdict

**Needs changes** — one should-fix in `README.md`. The `tui.c` behavior itself is good: I drove every journey the change touches and the restore works, with no doubling, no misplaced cursor, and no regression in plain mode. The README paragraph is much closer than last round — "usually" is honest, not weaselly, and the "older line" rewording is accurate. But the recovery hint, which is the one thing a stuck player has to act on, describes something the game does not do: **no** prout-terminated question is "asked again". Eight of the ten give a bare `Please answer with "Y" or "N":` with the question nowhere on screen, and the tenth (`destruct`'s password) treats the Enter as the answer and aborts the sequence. Only "a pause moves on" holds up.

## Findings

### 1. should-fix — `README.md:47-52`: "a question is asked again" is not what happens

Verified live. At `rest 5000` (the `prout`-terminated `Are you sure? ` in `events.c:420`), an 80x24 → 20x8 → 80x24 corner drag leaves the message area showing only a stale ` You will have 4 su`. Pressing Enter, as the README instructs, gives:

```
 Please answer with "Y" or "N":
```

The question is not restated. `ja()` (`sst.c:731-740`) loops on anything that isn't y/n, so the player is now answering a yes/no they cannot read. That covers eight of the ten affected prompts — including `deathray` ("are you sure this is wise?"), `mine`/crystal loading ("may risk a severe explosion"), the Treaty of Algeron violation, and the shuttle offer. A blind `Y` there is materially harmful.

The tenth is worse for the wording: `finish.c:24-26`'s password prompt reads with `scan()`, not `ja()`. I ran `destruct`, paged past the pager pause, squeezed to 20x8 and back — screen came back with the message area blank — and pressed Enter:

```
 PASSWORD-REJECTED;
 CONTINUITY-EFFECTED
```

Enter was taken as the answer and aborted the self-destruct. Neither "asked again" nor "a pause moves on".

Only `sst.c:775`'s announcement pause matches the text — `tui_getch()` (`tui.c:805-817`) returns any key but `KEY_RESIZE`, so Enter dismisses it. That half is right.

Suggestion: say what the player actually gets, and give them the safe way out. Every one of the eight `ja()` sites cancels on `N` (`return` / `Time = 0.0; return` / "don't take the shuttle"), and Enter at the password prompt also backs out, so "back out and give the command again" is uniformly true. Something like:

&gt; press Enter. A pause moves on. A question comes back only as `Please answer with "Y" or "N":`, without saying what was asked — answer `N` to back out of it and give the command again.

### 2. should-fix — same paragraph: the exception prompts can come back *cut short*, not only absent

The paragraph's observable test is "if the display comes back without a prompt on it". But these prompts are ordinary completed lines, so a width squeeze truncates them exactly like older text and the regrow leaves them truncated. At the same `rest 5000` prompt, 80x24 → 14x24 → 80x24:

```
 COMMAND&gt; res
 Are you sure
```

The game is still waiting. A prompt *is* on screen, so the README's test does not fire, and the player answers a yes/no they can only half read. This also means "an **older** line that was cut short stays cut short" understates: the live prompt does it too, in exactly the ten cases the next sentence is about.

Suggestion: widen the trigger to cover both shapes — "if the prompt is missing, or cut off mid-line, press Enter".

### 3. nit — `README.md:51-52`: "Every reading is a command away" is now in the wrong paragraph

The wording fix is right (readings are a command away; scrolled-away conversation is not). But it now closes the paragraph about *the line you are on*, when it answers the previous paragraph's "whatever scrolled away … is gone for good". Reading straight through, "press Enter: … a pause moves on. Every reading is a command away" is a topic jump. Suggest moving that sentence to the end of the preceding paragraph.

### 4. nit — `tui.c:463-477`: the new `else`-branch comment asserts something I reproduced in two resizes

The comment says the state that would show bottom-anchoring differing from last-row anchoring "has not been produced", on the reasoning that a failed search implies a full window, and cites instrumentation over "some forty height-only resizes without one counter-example". From a fresh `./build/sst -t` at 72x24: type the same wrapping answer the new test uses, drag to 72x14, drag back to 72x24:

```
13  mqqqq…qqqqj
14
…                       (nine blank rows)
23   Would you like a regular, tournament, or frozen game?regularbbbbbbbbbb
24   bbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbb
```

Bottom-anchored with the window plainly not full. Width was constant throughout, so this is the `oldmsgw == 0` branch. Player-visible but cosmetic, and still far better than the pre-fix behavior (which showed no question at all), so I would not hold the change for the code. The comment and issue #114's premise are what need correcting — as written they tell the next maintainer there is nothing to watch a fix improve, and there is.

### 5. nit — `tests/tui.sh`, wrapping-answer block: asserts the question, not the answer

The block counts the question and stops there. What a player is looking at after that grow is their half-typed answer; a change that lost it, doubled it, or left the cursor before it would pass this check. I confirmed by hand that the answer does survive intact across 72x30/36/42, so this is coverage, not a defect. Consider counting the full pair, or asserting the trailing `b` run appears exactly once.

Everything else the two new blocks assert matched what I saw: the setup question returns exactly once at row 14 after 20x8 and 10x8; `chart` does stop at `[HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]` at 80x24, returns exactly once after both squeezes, and Space really does page the chart on rather than being swallowed. The comment's claim about stumps eating the first keystroke I saw incidentally in a live run — a `c` eaten by a pager pause turned `chart` into `hart` and drew the whole command list.

### 6. nit (pre-existing) — `sst.doc:1541-1550` says nothing about resizing

The in-game `help` route to the manual describes the full-screen mode's existence and its fallback, but none of the resize behavior or the recovery. Not caused by this change; noting it since the README is currently the only place any of this is documented.

**Defensible, yes.** The trade is a self-healing cosmetic gap against `main`'s stump, where the question text is gone entirely and the reader is still waiting behind a fragment. Nothing is lost and nothing misleads. I would not hold the slice for it.

**The current code is byte-identical to `main` in that branch.** Comment-stripped diff of `tui.c` against `main` shows the whole executable difference inside `sync_size()` — `restore_curline()`'s `else` branch is untouched. Your two failed attempts left no residue.

Comment-stripped (`gcc -fpreprocessed -dD -E -P`) diff of `tui.c` against `main`, entire result:

```c
- int oldlines = builtlines, oldcols = builtcols;
+ int oldcols = builtcols;
- if (LINES &lt; oldlines || COLS &lt; oldcols)
-  restore_curline(COLS &lt; oldcols ? oldcols - 2 : 0);
+ restore_curline(COLS != oldcols ? oldcols - 2 : 0);
```

One behavioural change, in one function. Strictly it is two source lines, not one — the `oldlines` declaration goes too — but that is forced, since keeping it would trip `-Werror`. Nothing else in the file moves.

- **Both test blocks kill their mutants.** I copied the worktree twice outside the repo, configured fresh builds there, and ran `tests/tui.sh` against each:
  - Restoring `main`'s gate (`if (LINES &lt; oldlines || COLS &lt; oldcols)`) fails all four `pending:` assertions and nothing else in the file. The stumps it leaves are exactly what the test comment and the corrected #100 table claim: ` , or frozen game?`, ` CONTINUE]` at 20x8, and ` game?`, ` UE]` at 10x8 — so the "nothing at all" row in #100 really was wrong, and the leading space is the `mvwin(wmsg, PANELH, 1)` column offset, not a miscount.
  - Cutting the search to `int start = r;` fails `wrap: grown to 72x30 the question is not on screen exactly once` and nothing else, with two copies visible on the dump. The claim that this block is the sole killer holds.
- The two load-bearing README claims, against the code rather than the prose: `ja()` at `sst.c:731-740` loops on empty input printing only `Please answer with "Y" or "N":`, and `dstrct()` at `finish.c:25-31` compares the empty answer against `passwd`, fails, and returns — the sequence stands down. Empty input at `Units to fire=` (`battle.c:1115-1119`) also re-asks. The advice is safe.
- The two-line change itself is right. `restore_curline()` reaches the player only from `tui_readline`/`tui_getch`/`tui_gameover`/init, never mid-output, so the new grow call cannot fire while the game is writing. On a width grow the erase branch's `rows` is the stump's real footprint (curses truncates rather than reflows, so a pair that wrapped at the old width still occupies those rows), and `r = last - rows + 1` erases exactly that footprint. `oldmsgw` cannot reach the divisor as zero or negative. The joined-row reconstruction is exact because a pending pair always starts at column 0 (`note_out()` resets `curlen` at `\n` and `\r`).

## Findings

### should-fix — `tui.c:476-479`: the recorded reason for leaving the bottom-anchoring alone omits the thing that makes it correct, and points a reader at a defect

&gt; Erasing instead wants the row count the branch above computes, from a width that did not change -- a bigger change than a cosmetic gap is worth here.

The row count is not the obstacle. Since `COLS` did not change, `msgw` did not either, so `width = getmaxx(wmsg)` already *is* the old width and `rows = (total - 1) / width + 1` is one line away. As written, the comment says the fix is merely more work than it is worth — which invites the next reader to do it.

The actual obstacle is that `oldmsgw == 0` conflates two opposite states. On a height **grow** the stump is on screen and `last` names its last row, so erasing `rows` rows ending at `last` is right. On a height **shrink** `wresize()` truncated the pending line away entirely, so `last` names *live conversation* and erasing would destroy it. Concrete failure if someone acts on the comment: at 80x24 with the message window full, drag to 80x20; `last == maxy - 1` is a line of conversation, `rows == 1`, so the erase-anchored version wipes the line before the prompt instead of scrolling it up — whereas `wscrl(wmsg, 1)` preserves it. That is the branch's whole reason for scrolling, and it is the one fact neither the comment nor #114's second correction records.

Fix: add a sentence saying the branch serves both directions and that erasing is only safe in one of them, so a real fix has to distinguish them rather than copy the arithmetic. Worth mirroring on #114, whose comment 2 has the same gap ("computable here since the width did not change — but it is a real change to a branch with no test around it").

With that said: **leaving the gap is the right call.** I reproduced the mechanism from the code and it matches your account — on `main` that journey leaves a top-anchored stump with the question gone and no echo, so the new behaviour is strictly better in content and worse only in placement. Both attempted fixes are wrong for the reasons the comment gives. The rest of that comment is honest, including "it is this change that made it visible".

### nit — `tui.c:434-437`: duplicated sentence, and the surviving copy is now false

The block comment above `if (oldmsgw &gt; 0)` still ends with "A terminal that only lost rows is the other case: the line is gone altogether and the bottom rows hold older conversation worth keeping, so that scrolls by one and writes below it" — repeated verbatim as the first four lines of the new `else` comment at `462-466`. Two copies of the same claim five lines apart, and the outer one's "gone altogether" half is exactly what the new comment exists to correct: the branch is now also reached on a height grow, where the line is on screen as a stump. Delete the outer copy and leave the `else` block to speak for itself.

### nit — `tui.c:311`: "the one thing that leaves this false" is not the only thing

`cursor_at_prompt` is also left false when `sync_size()` returns at `if (!resized()) return;` — a `KEY_RESIZE` that arrives with no actual size change, which `tui_readline` can see because it clears the flag before calling `tui_refresh_panels()`. The behaviour is right (nothing was repainted, so the answer is still drawn and an echo would double it), but the comment claims an exhaustiveness it does not have, and this is a file where that has cost you three times. Either say "the one thing a repaint leaves this false for" or name the second case.

The post-`ui-review` edits you flag (comment corrections, the #115 assertion removal) are comment-and-test-only; I confirmed neither touches game output.

<result>## Verdict

The fix does what it sets out to do: at every prout-ended prompt I could reach, the question comes back on a resize, it is the *right* line, the answer lands on the row below it, backspace and Ctrl-U behave, and the waiting reader takes the answer. `tui_gameover` is clean — nothing stale reappears — and #100's journeys (part-written line, pending pause, wrapping answer on a grow) are intact, as is plain mode. **It needs changes before it ships**, though: the ended-line path deliberately drops `pending_answer` from its comparison, and the moment the player has typed *anything* before dragging, two visible faults appear — the question is duplicated once per width step, and on a one-row height shrink the typed answer is re-echoed straight over the question (" `yyy you sure?`"). Both are the failure modes the code's own comments say #100 existed to remove, reintroduced for the new case. README.md is also now wrong about these prompts and undersells the fix.

## Findings

**1. (should-fix, regression) A partly typed answer duplicates an ended prompt, once per width change.**
`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:428` — `want` is built as the prompt *without* `pending_answer` when `ended`. The rows in `joined` are blank-padded by curses, so the typed answer sits on the next row and the `tail_blank` test fails; the restore falls through to the rewrite branch and prints the question again.

Repro (80x24, `tournament 7 short novice pw`): `rest 5000`, wait for `Are you sure?`, type `y` **without** Enter, then drag 80→76→80 columns. Screen after two steps:

```
 COMMAND&gt; rest 5000
 Are you sure?
 Are you sure?
 Are you sure?
 y
```

A slow drag stacks one copy per step, exactly as the comment at `tui.c:422-427` describes for the curline case. Suggestion: build `want` for the ended case as the prompt padded out to the row boundary (`width - linelen % width` spaces) plus `pending_answer`, so it matches the padded `joined`. The same over/under-count is in the rewrite branch: `tui.c:490-494` adds `strlen(pending_answer)` to a single `total` even when the two are on separate rows, so `rows` is wrong there too — count prompt rows and answer rows separately when `ended`.

**2. (should-fix, regression) A one-row height shrink types the answer over the question.**
`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:469-471` — when the prompt is the last row of the message window, `i = (i / width + 1) * width` addresses row `maxy`; `wmove()` returns ERR and leaves the cursor at `(r, 0)`, where `row_blank()`/`mvwinnstr()` last parked it — the first column of the question. `cursor_at_prompt = TRUE` is set anyway, so `tui_readline` re-echoes the buffer from there.

Repro: `rest 5000`, type `yyy`, resize 80x24 → 80x23. Fixed build shows ` yyy you sure?`; the baseline (`main`) build under the same steps shows ` Are you sure?` intact. With nothing typed the two builds match (` nre you sure?` once you type a character) — that part is pre-existing — but the re-echo is new. Suggestion: when `start + i / width &gt; maxy - 1`, `wscrl(wmsg, 1)` and move to `(maxy - 1, 0)`; and only set `cursor_at_prompt` when the `wmove()` succeeded.

**3. (nit) At a one-row message window the restored prompt is written and immediately scrolled off.**
`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:549` — the `waddch(wmsg, '\n')` after the rewrite scrolls the line it just wrote off a one-row window. At 80x14 with `rest 5000` pending, the message area is blank and the game is waiting — the symptom #112 is about. Not a regression (the baseline shows nothing there either), but the fix could cover it: when `maxy == 1`, skip the newline and leave the cursor after the prompt, the way the curline path does.

**4. (nit) Only the last line of a multi-line question comes back, which can read as a statement.**
Inherent to keeping one line, and better than the nothing that came before, but worth knowing: at the self-destruct password the restored screen reads `SELF-DESTRUCT-SEQUENCE-WILL-BE-ABORTED` with a cursor under it — a player could read that as the outcome and type a command, which is then eaten as the password and does abort the sequence. `impulse` restores `we dare spend the time?"` and `deathray` restores `  are you sure this is wise?"`, both fragments. No change requested; mention it in the PR body.

**5. (should-fix) README.md is now wrong about these prompts.**
`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/README.md:47-52` still says "A few prompts finish their line before they stop to wait, and those get treated like any other older line — they scroll away, or come back cut off mid-sentence — while the game goes on waiting for the answer." I watched the baseline do exactly that (`SELF-DESTRUCT-SEQUENCE-WILL-B`, truncated) and the fixed build rewrite the same prompt whole and wrapped. The paragraph above it ("The line you are on usually comes back") should absorb the case, with a note that the answer to such a question is taken on the row below it. The "press Enter if the prompt is missing" fallback at line 53 is still sound advice and can stay. `sst.doc` says nothing about resizing, so it needs no change.

**6. (nit, coverage) The new case in `tests/tui.sh` cannot see findings 1-3.**
`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tests/tui.sh:1296-1345` never types before resizing, so the `pending_answer` interaction is untested; and its `80 14` iteration passes *through* a state where the question is on screen zero times without checking it. Adding a variant that sends `y` before the resize, and asserting the question is on screen exactly once at the intermediate size too, would have caught findings 1 and 3.

Built `Debug` from the working tree, plus a baseline binary from `main` in a scratch copy for before/after comparison. All TUI work in tmux on a private socket (`-L sstrev -f /dev/null`), no stdout redirection, cleaned up with `kill-server` on that socket only.

<result># Verdict: FAIL

Builds, the full suite and the plain-mode contract are all clean, and the narrowing you asked about **does hold**. But the one assertion you singled out — the never-more-than-one guard — **cannot fail at the shapes the block drives**, and I found a second, latent row-erase defect in the new erase arithmetic. Details below, with commands.

Note on inputs: the blob hashes in the task (`36b20e47…`, `85927648…`, `a6821ad3…`) do not match what is on disk. I verified against the working tree as it is now: `tui.c` `365b12bb`, `tests/tui.sh` `86f5c58f`, `README.md` `39ae9ae8`, branch at `72e87a2` (identical to `main`, so the whole change is uncommitted). ## Failures

### 1. `ended:` "a part-typed answer duplicated the question" cannot fail — deterministic

The block drives `20 8` and `80 14`. Both regressions the comment credits it with catching reproduce only at a **one-row height shrink** (80x24 → 80x23), which it never drives.

Two mutants, each a reconstruction of a regression named in `tui.c:418-429`, built and run outside the repo:

- **mutant C** — the "padded `want`" version: the `pending_answer != NULL &amp;&amp; *pending_answer != '\0'` early return deleted, padding kept.
- **mutant D** — the same, plus the answer dropped from the comparison for the ended case.

```
cd &lt;copy&gt;/mutC &amp;&amp; tests/tui.sh &lt;copy&gt;/mutC/build/sst Debug   -&gt; exit 0, 0 FAILs (2 runs)
cd &lt;copy&gt;/mutD &amp;&amp; tests/tui.sh &lt;copy&gt;/mutD/build/sst Debug   -&gt; exit 0, 0 FAILs (1 run)
```

The regression is real; the shape is wrong. Driving 80x24 → 80x23 → 80x24 by hand with `y` part-typed:

| build | 20x8 | 80x14 | **80x23** |
|---|---|---|---|
| working tree | 0 | 0 | **1** |
| mutant C | 1 | 1 | **2** |
| mutant D | 1 | 1 | **2** |

Mutant C's screen at 80x23 → 80x24, rows 21-23:

```
20:  COMMAND&gt; rest 5000
21:  Are you sure?
22:  Are you sure?
23:  y
```

At 20x8 and 80x14 the count is 0 or 1 for every build tested, so `n &gt; 1` is unreachable there. Adding an `80 23` step to the `for squeeze` list makes the assertion bite (both mutants go to 2). Deterministic, not flaky.

### 2. The ended-line erase counts one row too many when the prompt exactly fills a row

`tui.c:536-537`:

```c
if (ended &amp;&amp; pending_answer != NULL)
    total += oldmsgw - linelen % oldmsgw;
```

The matching padding for `want` at `tui.c:449` guards it — `ended &amp;&amp; linelen % width != 0 ? ... : 0` — this one does not. When `linelen % oldmsgw == 0` it adds a whole extra row, `rows` comes out one too high, and the erase anchored at `last` wipes a row of live conversation above the restored prompt.

Reproduced with 80x24 → 16x24 (message window 14 columns, `"Are you sure? "` is exactly 14) → 14x24. Working tree vs. a variant with the `% != 0` guard added:

```
working tree, at 14x24          guarded variant, at 14x24
21:  Are you suret               21:  COMMAND&gt; rest
22:  ?           ?               22:  Are you sure?
                                 23:  ?
```

The `COMMAND&gt; rest` line is gone and the prompt sits a row too high. `pending_answer` is a non-NULL empty string in exactly the untyped case this fix targets (`tui.c:856`, `buf[len]='\0'` with `len==0`), so the branch is live, not dead.

Latency: `linelen % oldmsgw == 0` needs `linelen &gt;= oldmsgw`, and the longest ended prompt in the tree is `finish.c:140` at 53 characters, so at the supported minimum (72 columns, `oldmsgw` 70) it is currently unreachable — it only bites below the minimum, or the day a longer ended prompt is added. Deterministic where reachable.

### 3. `README.md` and the `tui.c` comment overstate the #117 case

README: "the part you typed is on screen with the question above it possibly cut short." `tui.c:424-426`: "the answer is on screen, so the player can see what they typed."

Neither is true at any shape I drove. With `y` typed and the drag returned to 80x24:

- 80x23: the question is on screen, the `y` is **not** (message window row 24 blank).
- 80x14 and 20x8: the message window comes back **empty** — no question, no answer — with the reader still waiting. `Enter` then submits the invisible `y` and rests 5000 stardates.

This is not a regression — `main` (mutant A) is byte-identical in all three shapes — but the prose describes a state the game does not reach.

## What passed, and what I proved about it

**The narrowing holds.** No shape I drove duplicated or overtyped an ended prompt with an answer part typed: 20x8, 80x14, 80x23, 40x10, 72x14 all give count ≤ 1, and every one matches `main`'s output for the typed case exactly. The early return leaves `cursor_at_prompt` false, so `tui_readline` does not re-echo either.

**The untyped case restores** at 20x8, 80x14, 80x23, 40x24, 40x10, and starting from 72x24 as well as 80x24 — question back exactly once, `N` answered on the row below, `COMMAND&gt;` returned. Including the wrapping case the suite never reaches: `warp 1` / `move 1 1` gives `moving.c:594`'s 45-character prompt, which wraps at 40 columns and comes back whole on the grow, where `main` leaves the stump `Are you sure this i`.

**Assertions proved able to fail** (2 runs each, deterministic):

| assertion | mutant | result |
|---|---|---|
| `after 20x8 the question is not on screen exactly once` | A: `main`'s `tui.c` restored | fails |
| `after 80x14 the question is not on screen exactly once` | A | fails |
| `after 20x8 N did not answer the question` | E: repaint eats the player's next real keystroke — #112's own symptom | fails |
| `after 80x14 N did not answer the question` | E | fails |
| `rest did not ask a second time` | E | fails |
| `a part-typed answer duplicated the question` (both shapes) | C, D | **passes — see failure 1** |
| `rest did not ask whether it was wise` | — | not exercised (setup guard) |

Mutant A produces *only* the two expected failures — nothing else in `tui.sh` regressed, which is also the **#100 non-regression evidence**: the part-written line, the pending pause and the wrapping answer on a grow all pass on the working tree (3 runs, 0 failures) and are the assertions mutant A leaves green.

**`reader_waiting` is balanced on every path.** `tui_readline` sets it at `tui.c:831` and clears it at both exits — the `return TRUE` at 880 and the fall-through at 918, which is where the `c == ERR` and the bare-Ctrl-D `break`s land. `tui_getch` sets it at 928 and clears it at its single non-`KEY_RESIZE` return at 936; the `KEY_RESIZE` loop never leaves the function. No `setjmp`/`longjmp` anywhere in the tree, no nesting between the two readers, and `tui_gameover` (`tui.c:689`) is never called from inside one. `end_line`'s blank-line filter means `pause()`'s `"\r … \r"` wipe cannot install a blank `lastline`; `pause(0)`'s prompt is `proutn`, so it takes the part-written path unchanged.

**Everything else green:**

- `cmake -S . -B build-debug -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug &amp;&amp; cmake --build build-debug` — clean
- `cmake -S . -B build-release -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release &amp;&amp; cmake --build build-release` — clean
- Fresh `-DSST_WERROR=ON` Debug from scratch — clean, no warnings (`-Wall -Wextra -Wmissing-prototypes` are always on)
- `ctest --test-dir build-debug` — 11/11, 238s. `ctest --test-dir build-release` — 11/11, 230s
- `tests/tui.sh ./build-debug/sst Debug` run twice more standalone — 0 failures each (3 total runs, no flakes)
- Plain mode byte-identical to `main` on a seeded journey (`tournament 7 / short / novice / pw / srscan / status / chart / lrscan / damages / rest 1 / quit / n`), and the no-tty `-t` fallback likewise
- `gcc -fanalyzer -Wall -Wextra -Wmissing-prototypes -std=gnu17` over `tui.c` — clean
- ASan + UBSan Debug build driven through the whole of `tests/tui.sh` — 0 failures, no sanitizer reports (this is the check that covers `end_line`'s `memcpy`, the `%*s` padding into the 416-byte `want`, and the `joined` accumulation)

## Findings

### Blocker 1 — `end_line()` captures the pager's own prompt, so the restore puts the wrong prompt back over a live question

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:307` (`end_line`), reached via the gate at `tui.c:416`.

`pause()` (`sst.c:766-784`) writes `proutn("\n")`, then `proutn("[HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]")`, then after the keystroke `proutn("\r                           \r")`. `note_out()` calls `end_line()` on the **first** `\r` of that wipe, while `curline` still holds `[HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]` — non-blank, so it is stored into `lastline`. The trailing blanks are correctly rejected, but the prompt itself has already been recorded. The blank-run guard in the comment at `tui.c:293-296` stops the wrong half of the problem.

When the pager stops on a question's own `skip(1)` — `prout()` is `proutn(s); skip(1)`, and `skip()` calls `pause(0)` *instead of* emitting the newline — `lastline` ends up as the pager prompt and the reader that follows is `ja()`.

Reproduced (page height is `LINES-PANELH-1`, so 80x15 makes it 1):

```
80x15, "rest 5000", Space to clear the pager, ja() now waiting:
  Are you sure?
then resize 78x15:
  [HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]
```

The question is gone, replaced by a prompt that is not what the game is waiting for. Pressing Space there does nothing visible (it is echoed into the answer buffer); the player is told to press a key that has no effect while a y/n reader waits.

**This is a regression against `main`.** Same sequence on the `72e87a2` build leaves ` Are you sure?` on screen at both sizes. The change makes this shape strictly worse than the bug it fixes: #112 was a missing prompt, this is a false one.

Suggested fix: only `\n` should promote a line into `lastline`; `\r` should clear `curlen` without recording. That maps exactly onto the two cases — `prout()` ends its line with `\n` (the questions you want back), and `pause()`'s wipe is `\r` (the one you do not). Separately, `tui_clearmsg()` (`tui.c:955`) zeroes `curline`/`curlen` but not `lastline`/`lastlen`, so a line the game deliberately erased can still be resurrected; it should clear both.

### Blocker 2 — the README's recovery advice loses the player's game

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/README.md:48-55`.

&gt; Enter answers it with whatever you had typed, so if you cannot see what that was, the safe move is the one above -- answer `N` and give the command again.

The gap being described is a y/n question already part-answered, so the typed fragment is almost always exactly `y` or `n`. Following the advice literally:

```
"rest 5000", type y (no Enter), squeeze to 20x8 and back to 80x24
  -&gt; window shows " You will have 4 su" (a stump, not the question)
press N, Enter
  -&gt; "Your time has run out and the Federation has been conquered."
```

`N` is appended to the buffer, `scan()` lowercases the token to `yn`, and `ja()` (`sst.c:731-740`) tests `*citem == 'y'` — it answers **Yes**. The advice produces the destructive outcome in the exact scenario the paragraph exists to warn about.

Two further problems in the same sentence: "the safe move is the one above" points at the `N` advice that is in the paragraph *below*, not above; and "can mean an empty window" undersells it — at 20x8 the window comes back holding a misleading stump of old conversation, which is worse than empty.

The move that actually is safe is Ctrl-U (`tui.c:900`, which clears the line) then `N` — and Ctrl-U appears nowhere in `README.md` or `sst.doc`. That gap is pre-existing and fileable; the wrong advice is not, since this change introduces it.

### Should-fix 3 — at a one-row message window the found-path `wscrl` scrolls the prompt it just found off the screen

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:499-503`.

You guarded the *write* path with `if (ended &amp;&amp; maxy &gt; 1)` (`tui.c:599`), but the found path has the same hazard and no guard. With `maxy == 1`, `r = start + i/width` is `1`, `r &gt; maxy - 1` holds, and `wscrl(wmsg, 1)` discards the only row — which is the prompt that was just matched. `wmove(0,0)` then succeeds, so it returns claiming success.

`msgh = LINES-PANELH &gt; 1 ? LINES-PANELH : 1` (`tui.c:202`) makes `maxy == 1` for any terminal 14 rows or shorter, which is already in your test list.

Reproduced — message-window row at each step, `rest 5000` pending:

```
80x14   Are you sure?
78x14   (blank; reader still waiting, persists indefinitely)
76x14   Are you sure?
```

So a player dragging the width at a one-row message window loses the prompt on every other step. Not a regression (the `72e87a2` build never shows the question at `maxy == 1` — it shows ` You will have 4 supporting starbases.` at all three sizes), but it reintroduces #112's exact symptom at a shape this change itself creates.

Suggested fix: when `maxy &lt;= 1` there is no row below, so do not scroll — leave the cursor after the prompt on the single row, mirroring what `tui.c:599` already decides for the write path.

### Should-fix 4 — the test block's control assertion cannot fail at two of the three sizes, and misses the shapes above

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tests/tui.sh:1332-1341`.

```sh
tm send-keys -t "$pane" N Enter
sleep 1
if ! screen | grep -qF 'COMMAND&gt;'; then
        fail "ended: after $1x$2 N did not answer the question"
```

The comment says this "would fail a 'fix' that reprinted the question by re-asking it and left two readers stacked." At `80 23` it cannot fail at all: the echoed ` COMMAND&gt; rest 5000` survives the one-row shrink, so `COMMAND&gt;` is already on screen before `N` is sent. I confirmed this by capturing the pane at that point — `COMMAND&gt;` present, `N` not yet sent. Only the `20 8` case, where the deep squeeze wipes the window, makes it meaningful.

Given step 4's rule about assertions that cannot fail at the data they run over, this needs to assert something `N` actually produces — counting `COMMAND&gt;` occurrences and requiring the count to rise, or waiting for text only the post-`N` state prints.

Coverage gaps in the same block, both of which the findings above sit in: it never drives two consecutive resizes at a one-row message window (finding 3), and it never drives the pager-boundary case (finding 1), which needs a page height small enough that the pager stops on the question's own newline.

### Should-fix 5 — stale comment contradicting the change

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:344-352`. The `cursor_at_prompt` comment still reads "a prompt that ended its own line, which restore_curline() returns from at once and cannot put back at all. Issue #112." That is exactly what this change stops being true, and it cites the issue being fixed. In a file this heavily commented it will be read as current.

### Nit 6 — the ended erase clause is unreachable, and its comment describes a failure that cannot occur

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:534-542`.

The `#117` guard at `tui.c:428-429` means `pending_answer` in the ended path is either `NULL` or `""`. With `NULL` the clause is skipped; with `""` it raises `total` to the next multiple of `oldmsgw`, and `(k*w - 1)/w + 1 == (linelen - 1)/w + 1` — `rows` is unchanged either way. So the clause never alters the erase. The arithmetic is right for a future where #117 is fixed, which is a fine reason to keep it, but the first comment ("Added together they under-count and the erase leaves a row of the stump") states an observed failure that is currently unreachable, and two comment blocks sit stacked with no code between them. Merge them and say it is defensive against the #117 case.

### Nit 7 — the new `width &lt;= 0` early return also removes a fallback the non-ended path had

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:445`. Previously `width == 0` skipped only the search (`if (r &gt;= 0 &amp;&amp; width &gt; 0)`, still at `tui.c:457`) and fell through to the placement branch, which re-wrote the line. Now it returns without restoring anything, for the pre-existing partial-line case too. `msgw` is clamped to `&gt;= 1` at `tui.c:204` so it is likely unreachable, but the widened scope is unintentional and `width &gt; 0` at `tui.c:457` is now dead.

### Nit 8 — README overclaims for multi-line questions

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/README.md:48-49`, "those come back too". Only the last `prout` is kept in `lastline`. `moving.c:500-503` asks across three lines, so what returns is `we dare spend the time?"` on its own — better than nothing, but not the question.

## Review coverage

You told me both passes ran and what each found, which is what step 8 asks for on two of three points. The one you did not answer is the one step 8 singles out: **whether the last `ui-review` round saw the state being committed.** It did not, on your own account — the verifier findings came after it and three of the fixes were player-facing: the erase guard at `tui.c:541`, the `maxy &gt; 1` newline guard, and the rewritten `README.md` paragraph. Step 6 requires rerunning `ui-review` when a fix changes what the game shows, and rewriting the paragraph a player reads to recover from a lost prompt is squarely that.

Findings 1, 2 and 3 are all things a screen-reading pass on this state should have caught: a false prompt where a question was, a window that goes blank on alternate drag steps, and printed advice that ends the game. I read code and found them by driving the terminal; that is `ui-review`'s job, not mine, and the fact that I hit three of them from a cold read is the argument for the rerun rather than against it. Rerun `ui-review` on the fixed state before committing, and drive the one-row message window (14 rows or fewer) at more than one width, and a page height small enough that the pager stops on a question's own line.

## On the questions you asked

- **Is the narrowing right, or a half-fix?** The narrowing itself is well-judged — the `#117` gap leaves the player exactly where `main` left them, and the two alternatives you rejected both produce duplicated questions, which is worse. I would not widen it. But the README paragraph that documents the gap is where it goes wrong (finding 2), and the gap's real appearance is a misleading stump rather than an empty window.
- **Is `reader_waiting` sound as a gate?** As a gate on *when* to restore, yes — every exit from `tui_readline` and `tui_getch` clears it, `restore_curline` is only reached through `sync_size`/`tui_refresh_panels`, and `tui_gameover` is correctly excluded. The unsoundness is not in the gate but in what it gates: `lastline` is not guaranteed to be the prompt the reader is waiting on (finding 1). The gate answers "is someone waiting"; nothing answers "is this the line they are waiting at".
- **Is the ended-line arithmetic right where it survives?** The `want` padding, the `(i/width + 1) * width` cursor step and the `rows` computation all check out, including the case where the prompt exactly fills its row and curses' newline consumes an extra one — `(i/width + 1) * width` happens to track that correctly. The one place the arithmetic goes wrong is the unguarded scroll at `maxy == 1` (finding 3).
- **Does the test block assert what it claims?** The primary count assertion does and fails correctly pre-fix. The control does not, at two of three sizes (finding 4).
- **Should anything have been filed rather than fixed?** No — everything I found belongs to this slice. Ctrl-U being undocumented in `README.md` and `sst.doc` is the one thing worth filing separately; it is pre-existing, and it is also the answer finding 2's paragraph needs.

<result>## Verdict

The code side of this round holds up under driving: both blockers `code-review` found are genuinely gone, and the two faults the earlier rounds left (lost-on-alternate-steps at a one-row window, the row-too-many erase) do not reproduce across fine drags.
