## Findings

**1. should-fix — `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/README.md:61-67` contradicts the new paragraph at 56-59, and the losing advice is the one that comes last.**

Lines 56-59 say "Do not just answer it… Press Ctrl-U first." Line 61 then says, unqualified: "If the prompt is missing, or cut off, press Enter. Nothing waiting behind one commits you to anything." In the #117 shape the prompt *is* missing, and a part-typed `y` is still in the buffer, so Enter submits `y`. I drove it: `rest 5000` at 80x24, typed `y`, squeezed to 80x14 (window shows the stump ` You will have 4 supporting starbases.`), pressed Enter — the game went straight to

```
 Your time has run out and the Federation has been
 conquered.  Your starship is now Klingon property,
```

The claim "Nothing waiting behind one commits you to anything" is now false for the one case the page has just finished describing, and a player reading top-to-bottom takes the last, most imperative instruction. Suggest qualifying line 61, e.g. "If the prompt is missing, or cut off, and you have not started answering, press Enter" — or folding the Ctrl-U sentence into that paragraph so the two are read together.

**2. nit — README:49-50, the answer's placement is stated without the one-row exception.** "with the answer going on the row below the question rather than after it" is true at 15 rows and up; at 14 rows or fewer the message window is one row and the answer follows on the same row. Verified at 80x14: ` Are you sure? N`, and at 80x15/80x16 the answer correctly took the row below. The code deliberately does this (`if (ended &amp;&amp; maxy &gt; 1) waddch(wmsg, '\n')`), so the sentence is the only thing out of step. Suggest "…rather than after it — unless the window is down to its last row, where it follows on."

**3. nit — README:51 is 94 columns, against ~72 for the rest of the prose.** It is the only prose line in the file over 78 (lines 3, 5, 7 are badge/link lines). Reads as an unwrapped edit rather than a deliberate one.

**4. nit — the Ctrl-U recovery works but looks like the game breaking.** Following README:58 in the shape where the window comes back holding a stump, the keystrokes land on that stump: ` You will have 4 supporting starbases.` → Ctrl-U → `  ou will have 4 supporting starbases.` → typing `n` → ` nou will have 4 supporting starbases.` It clears on Enter and the recovery is correct (back at `COMMAND&gt;`, Stardate still 4800.0, no rest taken), but a player who has just been told the screen may be lying is now looking at a mangled sentence. One clause would cover it: "what you type lands on whatever the window happens to be showing; ignore it, it clears when you press Enter." Worth noting too that Ctrl-U appears nowhere else — not in `sst.doc`, not in `help` — so this paragraph is its only documentation.

**5. nit / candidate issue, not for this slice — a height shrink that clips the live ended prompt away lets the reprint overwrite a row of real conversation.** Three consecutive `rest 5000` asks at 80x24, then a squeeze to 78x20, gives:

```
 COMMAND&gt; rest 5000
 Are you sure?
 Are you sure?
```

The `n` that answered the middle ask was overwritten by the restored question, so it reads as though the game asked twice. `main` at the same shape shows the last line as `n` with no question at all and the reader silently waiting — i.e. #112 — so this is a net improvement and I would not hold the branch for it, but it is a real artifact of the erase branch assuming the bottom rows are the stump of the line being restored. Worth an issue.

**6. nit / below the supported floor — at widths under the prompt's own length the restored line renders as a fragment.** At 14x14 the message row reads ` ?           ?`; at 15x14 it comes back empty. These are 14-16 column terminals, far below the documented 72x24 minimum, and `main` merely leaves a stale line there. Reporting for completeness only.

## What I exercised

All tmux work was on private sockets (`-L uirev1/2/M/A/B`, `-f /dev/null`), each killed by name at the end; no bare `kill-server`. A `main` worktree was built separately for comparison and removed; the working tree is back to the three modified files with nothing added.

- **Pager boundary.** 80x15 (page height 1, two-row window): `rest 5000` prints the question, the pager stops on it, and after Space the question is what the reader waits on. Dragged 76/72/80/74/78 — exactly one `Are you sure?` at every width, zero `[HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]`. Repeated at 80x16/17/18 with the pause firing immediately before the question, dragged 76/72/80 — same result. Also dragged *while* the pager was up (80x15, 76x15, 80x14, 72x15): the pager prompt is restored each time and the question never is. Blocker 1 does not reproduce.
- **One-row message window across many widths.** 80x14, dragged one column at a time 79→65 then back through 70/75/80 (18 steps): `Are you sure?` present exactly once at every step, never lost, never doubled. Height drag at fixed width 23→14→24 plus 78x24/78x15 (16 steps): same.
- **Part-typed answer (#117 gate).** `y` typed, then nine shapes including 80x23, 80x15, 80x14, 76x14, 72x20 and back to 80x24: never more than one copy; the gap shows as a stump or an empty window, as documented.
- **Cursor landing.** After restore at 80x14, 80x15 and 80x16 the typed `N` lands after the question (one row) or on the row below it (two-plus rows), never over it. Same after growing back to 80x17 and after the self-destruct password prompt (`pw` on the row below `SELF-DESTRUCT-SEQUENCE-WILL-BE-ABORTED`).
- **README paragraph as a player.** Both routes driven end to end: Ctrl-U then `n` in the stump shape and in the blank-window shape (both recover, Stardate 4800.0, back at `COMMAND&gt;`); and the two things the page says that turn out to be wrong or unqualified (findings 1 and 2).
- **Other ended prompts.** `destruct` (three-line prompt, last line restored, dragged 80x14/76x14/72x14/78x14), `cloak on` (Treaty of Algeron, dragged 80x14/74x14), `quit` → "Do you want your score recorded?" and "Do you want to play again?" (dragged 80x14/76x14/72x20), the setup's first question before a game exists, and `ja()`'s `Please answer with "Y" or "N":` retry.

**Hashes verified.** The three digests you gave are sha256, and all three match byte for byte, unchanged before and after my review:

## The four judgements you asked for

**1. The `\n`-only rule is right, and it is complete.** I traced every carriage return the game can emit: `grep '\\r' *.c` finds exactly one writer, `sst.c:784`, which is `pause()`'s own wipe. The other candidate — `sst.doc`'s CRLF — is stripped at `sst.c:662` before `prout()` sees it, so a doc line never reaches `note_out()` with a `\r`. `note_out()` is the only recorder, and every write to `wmsg` outside `restore_curline()` goes through `tui_puts`/`tui_puts_slow`/the reader's own `note_out("\n")`. So `\n` promotes exactly the lines the game finished, and `\r` clears exactly the wipe. The rule holds.

**2. `lastline` can hold a non-prompt, but not a harmful one.** Two classes exist and I chased both:

- `note_out("\n")` at `tui.c:903` fires on the player's Enter while `curline` still holds the game's half-written prompt, so `lastline` becomes e.g. `"COMMAND&gt; "` and outlives the command. For that to be restored as a fake prompt, some later reader would have to wait with `curlen == 0` and nothing printed in between. I checked every `ja()` call site (`planets.c`, `battle.c`, `events.c`, `finish.c`, `sst.c`) — all thirteen print their question immediately before, and `scan()` only reaches `readinput()` after a `chew()`, which `ja()` does at entry. I found no reachable path. Not a finding, but it is the shape a future prompt-less read would break, and it is not what the `lastline` comment warns about.
- `lastline` as ordinary conversation (a `prout()` line followed by a prompt-less read). Benign by construction: nothing was printed after it, so it is still the last thing on screen and restoring it reproduces the screen rather than inventing a prompt.

The `reader_waiting` gate is airtight — both `tui_readline()` exits and the single `tui_getch()` exit clear it, there is no nesting, and `tui_clearmsg()` clears `lastline` so the `pause(1)`/`clearscreen()` path cannot resurrect an announcement.

**3. The one-row block asserts what it claims — measured, not taken on trust.** I copied the worktree twice outside the repo, configured fresh builds, and drove the exact sequence by hand on my own tmux socket:

| build | 78x14 | 76x14 | 74x14 | 72x14 | back at 80x24 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| good | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| mutant J (`maxy &gt; 1` relaxed) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **1** |
| mutant I (unconditional `wscrl`) | 0 | **1** | 0 | **1** | **1** |

Both mutants recover at 80x24, which is precisely why the pre-existing `ended` block passed them. The new block fails both at the first width step. Your verifier finding and its fix are correct, and starting the drag at 80x24 rather than at 80x14 is load-bearing exactly as the comment says.

I also confirmed the primary fix from the player's side: untouched, `Are you sure?` comes back once after a 20x8 corner drag (it read `Are you sure?` and nothing else).

**4. The README reads correctly top to bottom, with one overstatement.** The three paragraphs scope cleanly — "that one" in the Ctrl-U paragraph binds to "a question you have already started answering", and the Enter paragraph is now gated on "and you had not started answering it", so the losing instruction is no longer the last one a reader meets. I verified the central warning empirically: `y` (unseen) then `N` then Enter runs `rest 5000` to completion and ends with *"Your time has run out and the Federation has been conquered."* The warning is accurate and worth the paragraph.

**Build and tests on this exact state:** clean `-Werror` Debug build, `shellcheck -S warning tests/tui.sh` clean, `ctest` 11/11 including `tui` (115 s).

## Findings

### should-fix

**`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:333-337` — the surviving `note_out()` comment now states the opposite of the code, three lines above the code that contradicts it.**

```c
		/* A carriage return ends it as surely as a newline does:
		   pause() wipes its own prompt with "\r ... \r", and
		   ...  */
		if (*s == '\n')
			end_line();
		else if (*s == '\r')
			curlen = 0;	/* see end_line: a wipe, not a line */
```

Failure scenario: this is the comment a maintainer reads when deciding what `\r` should do here, and it says the two are equivalent — which is the licence for folding `\r` back into `end_line()` and reintroducing blocker 1 verbatim (the pager's `[HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]` restored over a question the game is really waiting on). The comment survives from before the change and was not updated with it. Fix: rewrite it to say the two are deliberately different and why — the `\r` case is a wipe whose bytes must not be recorded, the `\n` case is a finished line that must be.

### nit

**`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:287-306` — the comment block sits on the wrong declaration and says the same thing twice.** Its first line ("Ends the line and keeps it…") describes `end_line()`, but it is attached to `lastline`/`lastlen`; paragraphs 1 and 3 both explain `pause()`'s `"\r ... \r"`, and paragraph 2 is the one that actually documents `lastline`. Fix: lead with paragraph 2 on the declaration, move the `end_line()` rationale onto `end_line()` at line 317, and drop one of the two `\r` paragraphs.

**`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:360` — 82 columns, the only line in the file over 76.** `awk 'length&gt;76'` returns it and nothing else. Rewrap.

**`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tests/tui.sh:1358-1397` — the one-row block is inside `for squeeze` but never reads `$1`/`$2`, so it runs three identical times.** It hard-codes 80x14 and widths 78/76/74/72, and its failure messages name only `${w}x14`, so a real failure is reported three times identically with nothing distinguishing the runs. Costs roughly 25 s of the `tui` test's 115 s for no coverage. Fix: hoist it out of the loop.

**`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/README.md:62-63` — "ignore that; it clears when you press Enter" overstates what Enter clears.** Measured at 80x24 after a 20x8 squeeze with `y` part-typed: Ctrl-U rubbed out the `Y` of a live `You will have 4 su` line, `N` landed on top of it, and after Enter the row read ` N` — the newline cleared the text to the *right* of the cursor, but the character the player typed stayed on screen. Here that residue happens to look like a normal echo; where the cursor lands mid-line it will not. Fix: "the line it landed on clears when you press Enter, though what you typed stays where it fell." (Also `a `N`` at line 60 wants `an`.)

**`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/super-star-trek/tui.c:514-522` — the one-row branch puts the cursor at column 0 of the prompt when the prompt exactly fills the row.** With `maxy == 1` and `(found - joined) + linelen == width`, `i % width` is 0, so the cursor lands on the prompt's first character and the next keystroke overwrites it — the ` yyy you sure?` symptom the comment above it says it exists to prevent. Reachable: drag to 63x14 during a `pause(1)`, where `msgw` is 61 and `[IMPORTANT ANNOUNCEMENT ARRIVING -- HIT SPACE BAR TO CONTINUE]` is exactly 61 characters. Display-only — `buf` is unaffected, and `tui_getch()` does not echo, so at that one reachable size nothing is visibly wrong. Worth a line acknowledging the case rather than a fix; there is nowhere better to put a cursor in a one-row window.

## Review coverage

Your account holds up against the diff. The change plainly reaches the player, and `ui-review` saw the final state except for the last test-only block, which cannot. The coverage you named — the pager boundary at 80x15/16/17/18 across five widths, dragging with the pager up, both README routes end to end — maps onto what the diff can alter, and the one gap in that list (80x14, where the new `maxy &gt; 1` branches live) is covered by the new test block and by my own measurements above. `verifier` ran twice and `ctest` is green on the state I hashed. Nothing here is owed a pass that did not run.
